# Lanternlight ledger

The per-item record of what actually landed. One entry per item, **newest
first**, each carrying the acceptance evidence that justified calling it done.

This file exists because continuity in this project lives on disk, not in a
context window. A session that has been cleared or compacted reads the top few
entries here, plus `ROADMAP.md` and `git log`, and knows where it is. Nothing
about the work is expected to survive in conversation.

## Format

Each entry is a level-3 heading followed by its evidence:

```
### LL-0000 - YYYY-MM-DD - one-line summary of what changed

**Evidence:**
- the test, file, or command that proves it
- one line per piece of evidence
```

`ops/loop/ledger.py` writes these. It inserts new entries directly below the
marker line at the bottom of this preamble, and it writes atomically through a
temporary file, so a reader polling this file never sees a half-written entry.

## Append-only

Entries are added. They are never edited, reordered, reflowed or deleted. If an
entry turns out to be wrong, the correction is a **new entry** that says so and
names the entry it corrects. The value of a ledger is that it is a record; a
record that can be quietly revised - especially by an unattended loop - is
worth nothing.

## Do not verify an entry by its commit hash

Entries may cite a commit. Treat that citation as a hint, not as proof, and
expect a meaningful share of older hashes to resolve to nothing.

This is not history rewriting and it is not corruption. Work is done on
branches and in worktrees; when a branch is squashed on merge, or a commit is
cherry-picked onto another branch, the sha that existed when the entry was
written stops existing. The change still landed. Only its address moved.

**Verify by file and by test.** Open the file the entry names and read it; run
the test the entry names and watch it pass. Those survive squash, rebase,
cherry-pick and reclone. A hash does not. If a hash is dead and the file and
test check out, the entry is good - do not reopen the item, and do not "fix"
the history.

## Item ids

`LL-NNNN` is the convention. An id that appears in a roadmap item, a branch
name, a commit message and a ledger entry is what ties those four records to
each other, which is the whole reason ids exist.

**Two things this section used to claim are not true, and saying them anyway
hid a real defect.**

It said ids are "allocated in order". Nothing serialises allocation. Lanes work
on separate branches cut from a common base, so two lanes each asking "what is
the next free id?" get the same answer and both take it - and because each lane
appends only to its own `lanes/<lane_id>.LEDGER.md`, the two fragments merge
cleanly with nothing anywhere complaining. That happened on 2026-08-11: `ingest`
and `research` both took `LL-0023` for different work. Order is a convention the
integrator maintains by hand, not a property the machinery provides.

It said `LL-NNNN`, while the parser accepts any `### <id> - ` heading and the
safety lane's fragment had already used `SAF-0001` and `SAF-0002`, which
`integrate()` parsed without a murmur. Those two were renumbered by hand to
`LL-0026` and `LL-0027`. The format is still `LL-NNNN` and a fragment that uses
anything else will need renumbering before it lands here, but nothing enforces
it, so do not read a well-formed id as evidence of a checked one.

**"Never reused" is the part that is now enforced.** `ops.lane_state.integrate`
compares content, not just the id: an entry already present with the same text
is skipped, so the function stays idempotent and safe to re-run after a partial
merge, while an id present with DIFFERENT text raises `LedgerIdCollision` and
writes nothing. It will not renumber for you - the new id has to change in the
roadmap item, the branch and the commit message too, and quietly rewriting an
append-only record is its own defect. `ops.lane_state.duplicate_claims()` lists
every clash across this file and every lane fragment, so the collision can be
found before an integration rather than during one.

### LL-0115 - 2026-09-01 - Item 7c's two defence-in-depth gaps CLOSED - neither was a bug, and the tidy bound for the second would have refused a real comma at two of five crop offsets

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1415 passed', exit 0, run as its own command after the corrections
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0
- collect 1415 across 29 files, against a 1411 baseline measured before dispatching; ops.merge_gate.verify(baseline=1411) -> OK
- 1.0.15 set at crop origin (2058,390): 118 read, 6 refused, ZERO disagreements - identical to before the change
- crop-origin sweep y=388..392 after the change: 2/55/118/50/2 read and ZERO disagreements at EVERY offset; 0 of 231 panel-down frames produce a reading
- consumer diff over all 6,439 frames of the 2026-08-25 capture, HEAD against the change: 3,243 identical, 3,196 both refused, 0 gained, 0 LOST, 0 CHANGED
- refusal REASON shifts over those 6,439, outcome identical in every case: accept-band -> 'still wider' 5, grouping -> fragment 3, accept-band -> fragment 1, matched-no-digit -> 'still wider' 1
- frames at HEAD whose ONLY guard was _regroup: 3 of 6,439
- over-wide split pieces: 9 at the column-run level; under the full read_panel pipeline 6 reach the new postcondition and 3 refuse earlier on no-ink, a fragment and the bleed ceiling
- separator predicate populations, both captures: 85 genuine commas at width 3-4, first row 19-20, height 6-8, lit 12-14; 354 non-comma misfires at width 1-5, first row 12-26, height 1-8 with histogram 1x25 2x27 3x290 4x6 6x2 7x2 8x2
- comma geometry BY CROP OFFSET over the 55 four-digit frames: height 6-7 at y=389, 6-8 at y=390, 8-9 at y=391, 9 at y=392; first inked row slides 18-21
- four mutations, anchor asserted on each and the module restored byte-exact by sha256 after every one: removing the over-wide postcondition -> 2 RED, removing the too-narrow one -> 1 RED, removing the height floor -> 1 RED, raising the floor to 7 -> 2 RED including the four-digit read test

BOTH DEFENCE-IN-DEPTH GAPS RECORDED IN LL-0114 ARE CLOSED, and neither was a bug. No frame in 8,611 ever produced a wrong number through either gap, and the consumer diff over 6,439 frames shows 0 changed, 0 lost and 0 gained readings. What moved is which guard does the refusing: 10 frames shifted from a guard that can be retuned to one that is structural. THIS IS DEFENSIVE DEPTH, NOT A BUG FIX, and saying otherwise would inflate the work.
THE SPLITTER NOW OWNS ITS OWN POSTCONDITION. _split_merged validated nothing about the pieces it returned, so a 30-57px run - three merged glyphs, or scene bleed welding the row together - split once into pieces still 22-54px wide and went to the classifier anyway. Those were caught by the DISTANCE threshold alone at 0.1489 against an accept bound of 0.115: one guard where the design intends two, and the distance thresholds are the constants most likely to be retuned. The width and narrowness checks moved out of _read_field and into the splitter, which is the function that can actually vouch for them.
A COUNT THAT NEEDED ITS CRITERION PUBLISHED, and the same trap as last cycle. '9 over-wide pieces' is exact at the column-run level. Under the full read_panel pipeline only 6 reach the new guard, because 3 of those frames refuse earlier - one on no ink, one on a 5px fragment, one on the bleed ceiling at 939 lit pixels. Both counts are right about different questions, so the test pins all nine frames each with the reason it actually gives, rather than asserting a number that would have had to be fudged.
THE SEPARATOR RULE HAD A CEILING AND NO FLOOR, which is the whole of its looseness. A 1px speck low in the band passed as a thousands separator. Measured across both captures, 354 runs fire the predicate without being a comma and their heights are 1, 2, 3 and 4 against a genuine comma never shorter than 6. A floor removes 348 of the 354 and loses none of the 85 real commas.
THE TIDY BOUND IS A TRAP AND IT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT. Tightening the predicate to the comma population - top >= 19, height 6-8, width 3-4 - zeroes the misfires and REFUSES A REAL COMMA at crop origin y=391, and all 54 at y=392. The comma's geometry MOVES WITH THE CROP: height 6-7 at y=389 and 9 at y=392, first row sliding 18-21. So SEPARATOR_MIN_ROW and SEPARATOR_MAX_HEIGHT are buying CROP TOLERANCE, not discrimination, and tightening them would have destroyed the measured property that every offset in 388-392 yields zero disagreements. They are left alone and the docstring now says why.
THE FLOOR IS 5 AND NOT THE OBSERVED MINIMUM OF 6, because a bound set at the minimum ever seen has zero margin against the next capture, and this comma is already known to vary from 6 to 9 across crop offsets. NO INK-COUNT FLOOR IS SAFE AT ALL: a genuine comma at 9 lit pixels exists in the 2026-08-25 capture against a misfire population reaching 18, so the ranges overlap and any ink bound would cut a real comma.
THE CONSTANT IS PINNED FROM BOTH SIDES, which is the shape a tuned constant should have. Removing the floor fails the speck test; raising it to 7 fails the four-digit read test on real frames. A future pass cannot drift it in either direction without a named test going red.
TWO PREMISES IN THIS SESSION'S OWN BRIEF WERE REFUTED BY THE SLICE IT DISPATCHED. The brief asserted that none of the 384 misfires ever reaches a number; 32 of them are GENUINE COMMAS in frames that read a four-digit value, so the comma population is 85 rather than the 55 the docstring described - the 2026-08-25 capture has four-digit readings too, which only became true when the window was widened last cycle. The brief also implied the misfires were a live hazard; per-field, only 3 frames in 6,439 had _regroup as their only guard, and in every one the field held separators and NO DIGITS AT ALL, so none was ever within reach of placing a separator beside a digit. A BRIEF IS A HYPOTHESIS AND THE SLICE THAT ANSWERS IT SHOULD BE TOLD IT MAY REFUTE THE QUESTION.
THREE COUNTS OF THE SAME THING DIFFERED AND ALL THREE WERE RIGHT: the slice measured 87 commas and 381 misfires over 8,611 frames counting per-field instances and treating a comma as genuine even when another digit in its frame was damaged; the merger measured 85 and 354 over 6,563 frames requiring the frame to actually read. The slice's criterion is the better one and its numbers are the ones carried here for the population, while the merger's are carried for the blast radius. THE DIFFERENCE WAS RESOLVED BY PUBLISHING THE CRITERION, NOT BY PICKING A WINNER - LL-0113's rule applied a cycle later without having to be rediscovered.
ITEMS 7, 11 AND 12 REMAIN OPEN AND UNCREDITED. The client was closed for this whole cycle, confirmed by filtering on the process NAME. The loop lock was held against a live sentinel pid and the session watcher REFUSED because pid 17568 was already running, which is the correct result and not an error.

### LL-0114 - 2026-09-01 - CORRECTS LL-0112 a second time - the wrap refutation found a FALSE PRESENT-TENSE claim in the file that documents the reader, and the intermediate-tree measurement error for the THIRD consecutive cycle

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1411 passed', exit 0, run as its own command after the corrections
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0
- git blob hashes at this entry's commit, stated as GIT BLOBS because that is what a fresh clone reproduces - see the convention note below
- commit 6074631 adds 835 lines across 7 files, not the '790 added diff lines' across 'all four changed files' its own ledger bullet claims
- redact re-run over EVERY added line of 21f1c94..working tree: 995 lines, 0 non-ASCII, 0 email, 0 digit run of 8 or more, 0 flags other than one PERSONA false positive on an ordinary dict-comprehension line in the test file, whose identifier happens to be 14 characters - the repo's own file scan does not flag it and tests/test_no_pii.py passes over the whole 133-file set
- tests/test_vision_meter.py -vv -> 39 passed, ZERO skipped on this machine; the skip path proved real by redirecting PANEL/FULLSCREEN/TRANSCRIPTION to a missing directory, which skips 11 of the 39
- the case-insensitive sweep for 'nothing lands in 14-23' hits 4 tracked files; the sweep filed in LL-0113 required a capital N and found 3

A FALSE PRESENT-TENSE CLAIM SHIPPED IN THE FILE WHOSE PURPOSE IS TO RECORD THE READER'S BEHAVIOUR. The TestTheFullScreenCapture class docstring, added by 6074631, said 'It fails SAFE - every four-digit frame is refused and none is misread'. That was true when it was written, because it was written as the docstring of a FAILING acceptance test, and it was never updated when the behaviour changed in the same commit. A method 130 lines below in the same class says '54 of the 55 read'. THE FILE CONTRADICTED ITSELF BY 130 LINES AND THE FALSE HALF WAS THE CLASS DOCSTRING, which is the half a cold session reads first. Corrected here.
THE LESSON IS NARROWER THAN 'DE-STALE THE DOCS' AND IT IS NEW: A TEST WRITTEN TDD-FIRST CARRIES A DOCSTRING DESCRIBING THE BROKEN WORLD, and the commit that fixes the behaviour must revisit it. Nothing in this repo's discipline covered that case - the de-staling rules all point at ROADMAP, WAKEUP_NOTES and the ledger, and the defect landed in a test file that every prior sweep treated as code rather than as prose.
THE INTERMEDIATE-TREE ERROR, FOR THE THIRD CONSECUTIVE CYCLE. LL-0112's bullet 'redact.assert_clean over all 790 added diff lines' names a tree that was never committed. The sweep ran before the ledger entry, the hand-off document and the source-register fix were written, so it covered 790 of the commit's 835 added lines and 4 of its 7 files. 45 lines across 3 files were never covered by the sweep the bullet cites. LL-0109 recorded this exact shape ('795 added diff lines' against a shipped 1242) and LL-0111 recorded it again; recording it twice did not prevent it a third time, because the rule as written says re-measure before WRITING and the actual hazard is measuring before the LAST EDIT. THE OPERATIONAL FORM OF THE RULE IS: RUN THE REDACTION SWEEP AS THE FINAL ACT BEFORE 'git commit', NOT AS A STEP IN THE WORK. No leak resulted - all 995 added lines across the whole session re-swept clean here.
A HASH CONVENTION DEFECT INSIDE THE EVIDENCE THAT IS MEANT TO BE TAMPER-PROOF. LL-0112 quoted 'vision_meter.py sha256 ae2d6df8... at 20,752 bytes' and 'test_vision_meter.py sha256 b983144e... at 41,431 bytes'. The first is the GIT BLOB, LF-normalised; the second is the ON-DISK CRLF form. Both are true of something, neither was labelled, and .gitattributes pins *.py to eol=lf, so a fresh clone reproduces the first and CANNOT reproduce the second. This is CLAUDE.md's own 'Windows write_text turns LF into CRLF and byte counts lie' trap landing inside a ledger entry. THE CONVENTION FOR THIS REPO IS THE GIT BLOB, because it is the only form a clone can check, and any byte count or hash in a ledger entry must say which one it is.
AN AGENT'S NUMBER THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE, AGAIN, AND AGAIN BOTH WERE RIGHT. The wrap refutation measured 44 disagreements from swapping two VALUE template labels where LL-0112 recorded 77. Neither is wrong: the entry did not name WHICH pair was swapped, and a different pair was swapped. An unnamed parameter makes a number unreproducible even when it is correct, so the swap is now named - VALUE '1' against '7' gives 77.
WHAT THE WRAP REFUTATION CONFIRMED against the committed tree, independently and with its own harnesses: 1411 passed and ruff clean at HEAD and in a fresh clone; the collect baseline re-derived from 21f1c94 as 1390 across 29 files with only test_vision_meter.py changing, 18 to 39; 118 read, 6 refused, 0 disagreements, the refusal set frame for frame, 54 of 55 four-digit; all 231 panel-down rows still refusing; eight mutations each going red in the right place, including both tests the brief singled out; and every substantive figure of LL-0112 and LL-0113 reproduced exactly - the 18/309/743 population split, the 13px widest classifying run, x193, f0537's runs, guards-off at 123 with three wrong, the 6,439-frame consumer diff summing to 6,439, 384 separator firings, 9 over-wide split pieces at 0.1489, and the transcription sha256.
A PROCESS FAILURE IN THE WRAP ITSELF, DISCLOSED. The refutation was dispatched naming HEAD as ec3946d and two commits; by the time it ran, d638be2 and ad2209a had landed and HEAD was ad2209a with four commits. It caught the discrepancy itself and verified the real tree. Both extra commits were documentation-only so nothing was certified falsely - but a refuter handed a stale HEAD can verify the wrong tree and report green on it, which is LL-0111's rule failing in the very wrap that quotes it. FREEZE THE TREE BEFORE DISPATCHING THE FINAL PASS, OR PASS IT A COMMIT HASH IT IS REQUIRED TO CHECK RATHER THAN A NARRATIVE.
NOT RE-DERIVED, AND SAID SO RATHER THAN IMPLIED: the '0 of 121 newly-readable frames temporally inconsistent' figure was not re-checked by the refutation because LL-0112 does not state the neighbour-window criterion it used. The 121 and the presence of 441 among them were confirmed. A number whose criterion is unstated is not checkable, which is the same defect as the unnamed template swap above.

### LL-0113 - 2026-09-01 - CORRECTS LL-0112 - the width-gate evidence was scoped to ONE capture and written as universal, and the number that actually carries the gate is a different one

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1411 passed', exit 0, run as its own command after the corrections
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0
- the COMMITTED tree at 6074631 verified in a fresh git clone rather than in the working tree: '1411 passed', ruff clean, and core.hooksPath empty in the clone exactly as CLAUDE.md predicts
- runs of width 14-23 on the 6,439-frame 2026-08-25 capture, by window: VALUE 18, HITS 309, whole band unwindowed 743
- the widest run that ever CLASSIFIES as a digit anywhere in that capture: 13px, a '4' at x51-63 of p04331
- runs exceeding MAX_GLYPH_WIDTH 18 on that capture: 277, and the old-against-new consumer diff over the same 6,439 frames shows 0 disagreeing readings
- leftmost lit column inside HITS_WINDOW on the 2026-08-25 capture: x193, the window's own first column
- f0537 value-field runs: (54,65) (69,78) (82,92) for a true 618, so the failing glyph at x69-78 is the MIDDLE digit

LL-0112's EVIDENCE LINE 'merged runs 24-27px, NOTHING in 14-23' IS WITHDRAWN AS WRITTEN. It is true of the 1.0.15 capture and false of the 6,439-frame capture the module is regression-tested against, where 18 value-window runs and 309 hit-window runs land in that interval. The same sentence shipped in lanternlight/vision_meter.py and in ROADMAP.md, and both are corrected in place; the ledger is append-only so it is corrected here.
THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES AND ITS REAL SUPPORT IS A DIFFERENT NUMBER. The gate is sound not because the interval is empty but because THE WIDEST RUN THAT EVER CLASSIFIES AS A DIGIT IS 13px, so a gate at 18 sits 5px above the widest real glyph. 277 runs on that capture exceed 18 and reach the splitter, and none produced a wrong number. A count that rules an interval empty and a count of what the interval CONTAINS are different claims, and only the second one was ever load-bearing.
THE SAME DEFECT A SECOND TIME IN THE SAME COMMIT: 'no value run ever reaches x>=193, and the hit count starts at x199, so 84 columns of guaranteed-empty gap separate the two fields - widening cannot collide.' Also measured on 1.0.15 only. On the 2026-08-25 capture the leftmost lit column inside HITS_WINDOW is x193, the window's first column, so the region is not empty. The fields cannot collide because they are read through SEPARATE windows and the value window ends at 120 - a bound, not an emptiness. Both instances are the same error: A MEASUREMENT TAKEN ON ONE CAPTURE AND STATED AS A PROPERTY OF THE HUD.
A THIRD, SMALLER ERROR: three refusing frames were described as failing on their 'leading glyph'. True of f0527 at x54-66 and f0581 at x55-64, false of f0537, whose failing glyph is the MIDDLE digit at x69-78 - the '1' of 618. The merger's own measurement had printed x69-78 correctly earlier in the session and the prose was then written from memory, which is the LL-0111 failure repeating one cycle later.
AN ADVERSARIAL NUMBER THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE, AND BOTH FIGURES ARE RIGHT. The refuting pass reported 326 runs in 14-23; re-measuring gave 18. Neither is wrong: it counted BOTH fields, 18 value plus 309 hits, and the merger counted the value field alone. This is LL-0110's rule again - an exact measurement of a different question looks exactly like a refutation - and the fix was to publish the CRITERION beside each number rather than to pick a winner. The agent's substantive finding stood in full; only its scope needed naming.
WHAT THE FINAL PASS CONFIRMED, pointed at the committed tree as LL-0111 requires: 124 panel-up frames giving 118 readings and 6 refusals with ZERO disagreements, the refusal set frame for frame, zero wrong numbers across 2,172 plus 6,439 frames, an exhaustive brute force of the grouping rule, all 231 panel-down frames still refusing, and every guard load-bearing under mutation. It also found two latent gaps that never fired and are now recorded rather than fixed: a split piece is not re-checked against the width gate or recursively split, and the separator predicate is looser than the comma population it documents - it fires on any sub-6px run sitting low in the band, 384 times on the older capture, and none reached a number only because the grouping rule rejected them. Both are defence-in-depth gaps behind guards that did hold, so they are ROADMAP items rather than a hot fix.
THE PROCESS LESSON, since this is the second consecutive cycle with the same shape. LL-0111 said to re-measure every figure immediately before writing it. That was done for the acceptance figures and NOT for the supporting ones - the population bounds were carried from a slice report written ninety minutes earlier against a different capture. RE-MEASURING THE HEADLINE NUMBER IS NOT RE-MEASURING THE EVIDENCE, and a supporting number is exactly where a scope silently drops.

- python -m pytest -> '1411 passed', exit 0, run as its own command AFTER the document edits
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0, run as its own command

- shipped hashes: lanternlight/vision_meter.py sha256 ae2d6df8362119cd... at 20,752 bytes; tests/test_vision_meter.py sha256 b983144e4b037825... at 41,431 bytes
- vision_meter.read_panel over all 124 panel-up frames of C:/ll-captures/2026-08-30/frames at crop origin (2058,390): 118 read, 6 refused, ZERO disagreements; four-digit 54 of 55; digit tally 20/7/42/55 unchanged
- the human transcription was single-copy in a volatile temp scratchpad and is now preserved at C:/ll-captures/2026-08-30/meter_transcription_cycle34.csv, byte-identical by sha256 973a3f58... across source and both copies
- consumer diff over all 6,439 frames of the 2026-08-25 reference capture, old module against new: 3,122 identical readings, 3,196 both refused, 121 NEW reads where the old refused, 0 readings lost, 0 disagreeing
- the 121 newly-readable frames have no transcription, so they were checked for temporal consistency instead: 0 of 121 inconsistent with their nearest read neighbours
- guards disabled (accept band and ambiguity margin): 123 of 124 read and THREE wrong - 262 for 261, 633 for 618, 3334 for 1834
- measured populations: single-glyph runs 8-13px value and 10-12px hits, merged runs 24-27px, NOTHING in 14-23; separator first-inked-row 19-20 against digit 4-6; separator width 3-4, height 6-8
- 19 merged runs, 18 with a leading '4'; 12 definitely-single glyphs carry an interior blank column, 11 of them the digit '0'
- redact over all 790 added diff lines: 0 non-ASCII, 0 email, 0 digit run of 8 or more; tests/test_no_pii.py passes and all four changed files are in its 133-file scan set, confirmed by listing rather than assumed

ITEM 7c's ORANGE PAIR IS CLOSED AND THE WHITE ROW IS NOT. The blind-at-1000 gap is shut: 61 read at the start of the cycle, 118 now, zero disagreements throughout. MIN_GLYPH_WIDTH was NOT lowered. The fix was VALUE_WINDOW (48,92) -> (40,120), a thousands-separator rule keyed on the run's FIRST INKED ROW, a merged-run splitter gated on a new MAX_GLYPH_WIDTH of 18, and a grouping check that refuses a malformed thousands group.
THE ITEM'S OWN PRESCRIBED THIRD FIX WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN. ROADMAP 7c said the sub-1000 refusals were "the signature of templates harvested from a different capture" and prescribed re-harvesting. Following LL-0100, what that claim MEASURED was established before overturning it: it measured the distances, 0.1352 to 0.1644, and those reproduce exactly. The inference fails on a positive control - the ORIGINAL 2026-08-25 capture, the templates' OWN source, produces accept-band refusals in the SAME 0.122-0.165 band on the same 12px and 24-27px runs. A band that appears in the templates' own source capture cannot be a signature of staleness. Glyph geometry is identical across both captures and the new capture is if anything safer, at max distance 0.0871 against 0.0925 and tightest margin 0.0638 against 0.0318. THE REAL THIRD PART WAS SEGMENTATION, NOT TEMPLATES, and re-harvesting would have risked poisoning the set with dithered transition frames.
THE ACCEPTANCE SAID "ALL 124" AND IT IS NOT MET, for a reason that is the result rather than an excuse. f0661 carries ZERO orange pixels anywhere in the row band - the transcription itself flags it not legible and a human read it at 8x - so no window, template or threshold can extract a digit from it, and its refusal is the required "panel is not up" behaviour. "All 124" was never achievable.
THE OTHER FIVE REFUSALS ARE THE MODULE WORKING, AND THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED. It would be easy to read them as a timid threshold and widen a constant until 124 of 124 came back. Turning off the accept band and the ambiguity margin does exactly that and returns 123 of 124 - with THREE wrong, including 3334 for a true 1834, wrong in its LEADING digit by 1500 in a damage series. On f0581 the two candidates tie at 0.165 to three decimals and the true digit is neither. A test now pins that, so a future pass cannot widen a constant to chase the remainder without going red. THE COUNT THAT MATTERS IS NOT HOW MANY FRAMES READ, IT IS THAT NONE IS MISREAD.
A RECORDED CLAIM IS WITHDRAWN AND IT WAS FOUND BY DIFFING THE CONSUMER'S OUTPUT. ROADMAP 7c and tests/test_vision_meter.py both recorded that hit 8 of the second hand-read series - 441 - "is genuinely not in the capture at this cadence". It is at p01216_19.02.51.472.png and p01217_19.02.52.028.png, both reading 441 / 8 Hit and sitting exactly between 386/7 and 496/9. It was never absent: 441's '4' and '1' merge into one 24px run and the reader of the day refused those frames. What the claim was MEASURING was that the reader of 2026-08-27b could not produce 441, which was true of that reader; the error was generalising from "the reader cannot read it" to "it is not in the capture" - a claim about the INSTRUMENT written down as a claim about the DATA. The second series is now pinned COMPLETE at all ten values. This repo's "an empty search is a claim about the search" rule, arriving from a new direction: A REFUSAL IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE READER.
PROVING THE CHANGE HAPPENED IS NOT PROVING IT MATTERS, so the consumer's output was diffed rather than the edited lines. Over all 6,439 reference frames the change is monotone: no reading changed value, no coverage was lost, and 121 frames that used to refuse now read. That diff is what surfaced 441.
A MUTATION FOUND A DEFECT IN THIS SESSION'S OWN NEW TESTS. Deleting the separator's row rule entirely left the suite GREEN. The high-fragment test painted a run 19 rows tall, so the HEIGHT check refused it and the row rule - the whole point of the design - was never exercised. A 7-row test, a comma's exact height differing only in sitting high in the band, now goes red for that mutation. Six further mutations kill the height rule, the grouping rule, the split gap-count rule, the width gate (8 tests, including the committed fixture), the window revert, and a swap of two VALUE template labels, which produces 77 disagreements and is what proves the zero-disagreement test is loud rather than decorative. The module was restored byte-exact by sha256 after every one, and each mutation asserted its anchor matched before the run, because a mutation that fails to apply looks exactly like a passing test.
THE SPLITTER IS GATED ON WIDTH, NOT ON THE VALLEY, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS THE SAFETY. Every one of the 19 merged runs has exactly one interior blank column at its minimum, so a valley rule looks sufficient - but 12 definitely-single glyphs carry an interior blank column of their own, 11 of them the digit '0' whose hollow centre looks exactly like a join. A valley-only rule would shred nine frames that currently read correctly. The measured populations are disjoint with an 11-column gap and nothing at all in 14-23, and both directions of a mis-set gate fail safe.
THE GROUND TRUTH WAS SINGLE-COPY IN A TEMP DIRECTORY. The human transcription this item's acceptance is measured against existed only in cycle 34's session scratchpad, which is volatile. It is now preserved in the capture tree, verified byte-identical by sha256. An acceptance criterion whose ground truth can be garbage-collected is not an acceptance criterion.
ITEMS 7, 11 AND 12 REMAIN OPEN AND UNCREDITED. Nothing here touches them. Item 12's backward comparison stays closed as impossible and its forward acceptance stays open; item 11 still has 101 and 214 unbound.
STILL OWED AND UNCHANGED: the Splatter Arrow tooltip, and one of Elusive, Smiting or Curse for the five-level ladder prediction. Both need a hover and the client was closed throughout.
TWO DECISION GATES RECORDED RATHER THAN TAKEN. A registration search would recover f0469 and f0470, whose panel is still sliding in - a +2px shift scores 0.0601 at margin 0.0910 - but it searches for an alignment that makes a glyph match, which multiplies scoring attempts and erodes the very margin guarantee this module rests on, so it is written into ROADMAP 7c with an acceptance criterion instead of being taken unilaterally. And a four-digit committed fixture would let a fresh clone verify the separator and splitter paths against real pixels rather than synthesised masks, but the LL-0083 precedent is that capture-derived pixels enter this public repository only on the operator's explicit approval, and the operator is playing the game.
NOTHING WAS DONE TO THE GAME OR ITS DIRECTORY. The client was closed throughout, confirmed by filtering on the process NAME rather than a command line, which is the LL-0105 self-match trap. ensure_armed was called with the BASE path and refused on its own - pid 17568, running since 07:50:14 local - which is the correct result. No raw log excerpt is committed.

IT IS THE EXACT DEFECT THE SAME COMMIT ADVERTISES AS CAUGHT, one paragraph away. Worse, the correct decomposition had already been derived during the session and was then not written down - the arithmetic was done and the sentence was written from memory. FIXING AN INSTANCE IS NOT FIXING THE CLASS: when a defect is found, re-run the corrected measurement over every actor the document describes, not just the one that surfaced it.
THREE OF LL-0110's EVIDENCE NUMBERS DO NOT REPRODUCE, and the substance behind each survives. '795 added diff lines' was measured on an intermediate tree; the shipped commit adds 1242, and redact.assert_clean passes over all 1242 with zero non-ASCII, zero IPv4, zero email and no digit run of 8 or more except the two client Build Dates. THE PATTERN ACROSS ALL FOUR IS ONE PATTERN: a number measured at one moment and quoted at a later one, after the thing it described had changed. A count is only true of the tree it was taken from, and a wrap edits the tree after the count. RE-MEASURE EVERY FIGURE IN AN ACCEPTANCE RECORD IMMEDIATELY BEFORE WRITING IT, not during the work it describes.

The anti-cheat boundary is clean, with the patterns positive-controlled.
ITEMS 7, 11 AND 12 REMAIN OPEN AND UNCREDITED. Item 12's backward comparison stays closed as impossible and its forward acceptance stays open.

WHAT CROSSED THE PATCH BOUNDARY INTACT, which the item said was worth as much as an overturn: the display model, reproduced by R1's non-empty rounding solve against an empty truncation solve; the standard bot on every recorded field; and the safe-circle radius. FINDINGS 13.1's 'a loadout baseline can only be pixels' is REFUTED, and the failure is instructive. OnCustomInfoReady roleInfo fires within a second of every TrainingGround LoadMap carrying the full equipped set with affixes, gems and durability, plus talents, class, level and exp. 13.1 measured that the .sav files carry no equipment, concluded correctly that equipment is server-side, and then generalised to the whole local surface WITHOUT GREPPING THE LOG. The save measurements stand; only the last inference fails. A NEGATIVE OVER ONE SURFACE IS NOT A NEGATIVE OVER THE MACHINE.
THE TRAINING ROOM SETTINGS PANEL HAS FIELDS AND NOBODY HAD READ THEM. Bot Settings: Weapon Archetype Blackarrow, Difficulty 1, Equipment Rarity Common Equipment. They configure the TARGET, so they parameterise every damage number the room has ever produced. This repo has recorded the panel's ENGINE names since 2026-08-25 and never once opened it. Two of the three are positive-controlled against log facts; Difficulty is recorded UNMAPPED. A RENDERED PANEL'S NAME IS NOT ITS CONTENTS.

FINDINGS 11.7's constancy rule is NARROWED, not refuted. It offers constancy as the diagnostic for floor membership. On 1.0.15 two segments of one visit each admit a constant, at about 57.9 and about 8.65, and they cannot both be a minimum. The likely reconciliation, never excluded, is that constancy indicates a STATIONARY SHOOTER rather than a clamp. A probe built to test that from inter-frame scene change FAILED - it cannot separate camera movement from animation and arrows in flight - and is reported as a failed probe rather than as weak evidence.
SIX DEFECTS IN THIS SESSION'S OWN ADDITIONS WERE CAUGHT BY THE REFUTATION BEFORE THE COMMIT, and four were the merger's. 'Eight of nine equipment slots differ' was nine of nine and had already propagated into two documents. 'Every durability 1400' dropped 2 zero-durability items per bot because the extractor kept only TRUTHY durabilities - a truthiness test is not a presence test, and it is the same defect as an earlier pass reading a key named gems instead of gem. '16 spawns in 1.0.14 A' was 20. A claim that 1430301 was worn in only one session was wrong - it is worn briefly in a second. A bracketing note conflated the talent data response surface with roleInfo, which disagree INSIDE one session and together bracket the allocation to between 21:56:22 and 22:49:35. And a paragraph titled '10.35 CARRIES A SCOPE' said 'against the training bot' while that session spawned two kinds.
A DISAGREEMENT THAT WAS NOT AN ERROR, and the distinction is the point. The refutation reported '124 panel frames is 123' and '3 of 20 single-digit frames is 2 of 19'. Both were exact, and both answer a different question: the single frame between them is f0661_00.45.40, motion-blurred, ZERO ink in the module's row band, and legible as 9 / 1 Hit at 8x. 124 means a human could read it; 123 means a detector fired. The number was not changed - the CRITERION was published beside it. AN EXACT MEASUREMENT OF A DIFFERENT QUESTION LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A REFUTATION, which is LL-0100's rule arriving from the other direction.
grep -iF CRASHES ON THIS MACHINE and looks exactly like a clean negative. GNU grep 3.0 under Git Bash: grep -i matches, grep -F matches, together they abort with SIGABRT and exit 134. Under the 2>/dev/null that most sweeps carry, the crash is invisible and the empty output reads as 'no matches' - it nearly passed a withdrawal check on a crashed instrument. Recorded in CLAUDE.md along with the companion rule that a LINE-oriented grep is a claim about the file's line breaks, since prose here wraps near 80 columns and two checks this session returned false clean bills that way, one of them on item 12's own false-premise clause.
vision_meter GOES BLIND AT 1000 AND FAILS SAFE. Above 999 the meter renders a thousands comma, which is a 3 px column run inside the module's row band against MIN_GLYPH_WIDTH 6, so every four-digit frame is REFUSED and none is misread. The merger's own earlier probe measured that comma at 5 px and inferred it would be silently dropped - measuring its OWN crop rather than the module's band. The hedge attached to that probe is the only reason this is a refinement and not a retraction. The fix is to re-harvest templates, NOT to lower MIN_GLYPH_WIDTH, which would convert a safe refusal into a silent truncation.
THE FIX THIS SESSION PRESCRIBED FOR 7c WAS INSUFFICIENT AND WOULD HAVE CAUSED THE EXACT TRUNCATION IT FORBADE. The draft said 'teach the reader a separator glyph and do NOT lower MIN_GLYPH_WIDTH'. An adversarial pass implemented precisely that and got 2,06 from a true 2,000, because VALUE_GLYPH width was never the issue - VALUE_WINDOW is (48,92), sized for three digits, and it CLIPS the last two digits of a four-digit value. The 3 px refusal is currently the ONLY thing standing between this module and a confident wrong number, so it must not be removed before the window is widened. The real fix is all three together: widen the window, teach the separator, re-harvest templates, then re-run the 124-frame set expecting zero disagreements. WRITING DOWN A CAUTION IS NOT THE SAME AS HAVING CHECKED THE THING THE CAUTION IS ABOUT.
A SECOND RELAYED CLAIM FAILED THE SAME WAY. 'Row 390 is the only row that works at all' came from an agent's report and was published without measurement. Swept over five crop origins it is the BEST row, not the only one - 2, 33, 61, 37 and 2 correct readings at y=388 to 392 - with ZERO disagreements at every offset. The corrected fact is stronger than the one it replaces: vertical misalignment degrades to refusal and never to error.
AN ORCHESTRATION ERROR, disclosed because it destroyed files. All three parallel slices were given ONE shared scratchpad directory and collided; two files were overwritten and an agent then deleted two it believed were its own. Nothing of value was lost and no repo file was touched. Disjoint FILE SETS were applied to the repo and not to the one directory every slice was told to write into. Give every parallel slice its own subdirectory.
STILL OWED, and the negative is now POSITIVE-CONTROLLED AND BROAD: the Splatter Arrow tooltip, and one of Elusive, Smiting or Curse for the five-level ladder prediction. A first draft asserted the log carries no such names from SIX words, which an adversarial pass correctly called a generalisation. Re-tested at 33 known rendered names - 20 affix, 8 skill and talent, 5 item - with Steel Arrow and Sky Piercer as controls: every skill, talent and item name scores zero, and the four affix words that hit are all confounds - UI focus routing, an AllowPIESeamlessTravel CVar, the RangedAttackIndicator module, and a lowercase wealth CURRENCY field in match results that a careless grep would have counted as Wealth. So the log carries no player-facing affix, skill or item name, and no log work substitutes for a hover.
FOUR SMALLER CORRECTIONS FROM THE SAME PASS, all re-measured before accepting: window B's max inter-frame gap is 2 s, not the 3 s that table had copied from window A; f0462_18.47.18 is a 1280x720 JPEG and the only copy on disk, so calling it a full-resolution frame was wrong; and the log-corpus inflation is 6.25x counting files or 3.5x stopping at distinct contents, where a draft had written the remembered range 'four to six times', which is neither.
ITEMS 7 AND 11 ARE NOT CREDITED. Item 11 still has 101 and 214 unbound. 101's blocker is now exact rather than approximate: the item and the screen never coincide, and the recipe is checkable before any frame is read by grepping the session log for WBP_EquipSkill_DetailPrompt.

THE 8 PERCENT FIGURE WAS WRONG BY A FACTOR OF FOUR AND IT IS THE COSTLIEST DEFECT OF THE SESSION, because it is a wrong 'do not look here'. LL-0107 and the hand-off notes said only 8 percent of tooltip cfgId events have any frame within two seconds. Re-measured over every naming convention it is about 39 percent. THE CAUSE IS THE SAME OMISSION THAT HID A DECIDING FRAME EARLIER THE SAME DAY: the index pattern matched f and p filenames but not s, silently dropping the 2,683-frame scene sets. It repeated INSIDE the measurement written to warn about it, and the rider 'six times more frames did not move it' was false for the same reason. Corrected in the hand-off notes; LL-0107 stands as written because the ledger is append-only and this entry is its correction.
THE WORLD-SPLIT COUNTS WERE BYTE-PREFIX INFLATED, and the entry that published them carries the rule they break. LL-0107 reported CampMap 1690 of 1690, Whitewoods_Day 0 of 1246, Training 0 of 59, counted over 14 distinct log CONTENTS. Only 4 of those 14 are not strict byte prefixes of another, and on maximal logs the counts are 863, 379 and 20. THE RATIO SURVIVES AND THE COUNTS DO NOT - which is the useful half, since the operational rule was always 'the join is total in CampMap and empty elsewhere'. DEDUPLICATING BY CONTENT HASH IS NOT ENOUGH; a growing log yields many distinct hashes for one session.
A CLAIM WITHDRAWN IN ONE PARAGRAPH WAS RESTATED 90 LINES LOWER IN THE SAME ITEM. ROADMAP item 4d withdrew 'arming is bolted to the one step a cycle cannot skip', and then its own closing paragraph ended 'Arming now rides on the step a cycle cannot skip'. Verified in code: released() in the guard module contains no arming of any kind. THIS IS EXACTLY THE DEFECT LL-0104 NAMED - a stale sentence surviving a few hundred lines from its correction - recurring inside the item that named it. A withdrawal is not done until you grep the withdrawn phrase across the file that carries it.
TIER 5 WAS MEASURED ALL ALONG AND MY PARSER COULD NOT SEE IT. The tier rule was published as exact at tiers 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6, with 5 silently absent. Seven cfgIds log durability 1400, exactly 900 + 5 * 100. My extractor anchored on a brace-balanced object beginning {"id":"<digits>" and those records are in another shape, so a NEGATIVE about tier 5 was a claim about my parser. The rule now reads 'every tier 1 to 6' and is stronger than what was published.
THE SLOT RULE WAS TRUE AND OVERSTATED. 'The second cfgId digit is the equipment slot' is contradicted by the table printed directly under it - weapons are 30xxxxx at slots 10 and 11, second digit 0 - and by the gem ids two sections above, which have second digit 2 and are not slots at all. Scoped to 1xxxxxx ARMOUR it holds on 917 payload lines with zero violations, which is a far better control than the two items originally cited. A RULE STATED WITHOUT ITS SCOPE IS REFUTED BY ITS OWN EXAMPLE TABLE.
A GEM WAS ATTRIBUTED TO THE WRONG SOCKET. AFFIXES said the weapon's Fervor was 'rendered in its Peridot row'. On that frame the Tier II Peridot Slot reads EMPTY and the Fervor row sits separately with a teal icon, which this document's own gem taxonomy calls a Moonstone. The binding is unaffected - the log gives that weapon one empty socket and gem 223106 - but the sentence contradicted a taxonomy the same file established.
THE DE-STALING SWEEP EARLIER IN THIS WRAP MISSED ITS OWN CLASS OF DEFECT. It searched for recitals saying FOUR affix ids remain and fixed four documents. A hand-off entry from cycle 32 says THREE, with a live recipe for an id bound since. A SWEEP KEYED ON THE OLD VALUE MISSES EVERY OTHER OLD VALUE - sweep on the CLAIM, not on the number it happened to carry last.
A NEW VACUOUS GUARD IS DISCLOSED RATHER THAN CLAIMED AWAY. In tests/test_armwatch.py, test_the_default_clock_is_the_local_one stays GREEN when the local clock call it protects is swapped for a UTC one, because it can only discriminate between 19:00 and 23:59 local. Its docstring claims otherwise. The property IS pinned elsewhere - stripping the timezone conversion from the ops-side resolver reddens a different test - so the behaviour is guarded and only this one assertion is decoration. Written down, not quietly left.
WHAT SURVIVED THE AUDIT UNBROKEN, recorded so it is not re-litigated: both affix bindings and their reasoning, checked against an explicit search for an alternative assignment and none exists; the two gem ids; the claim that exactly two Affix Details openings exist in the corpus and both are frame-covered; every item-7 count re-derived by raw regex over save bytes bypassing the repo reader entirely - 353 saves, 278 top-level, 424 child, nameId zero on all 424; the lowercase-nameid surface with its determinism table; the 262 ms hover; every capture-tree cost figure; the watcher process; ASCII and line endings on all changed files; zero operator PII in a 193 KB diff across seven proved patterns; and the anti-cheat boundary. Eight guard mutations went red in the right tests.
THE PATTERN ACROSS NINE INSTRUMENT ERRORS THIS SESSION IS ONE PATTERN. A clock harness that armed twice, a clamped fake clock, a window scan reading string bytes as an integer, a malformed awk range, a frame index missing a naming convention, a brace-blind regex finding zero records where 131 exist, a process probe matching itself, a set difference with two valid solutions, and now a parser that could not see tier 5. EVERY ONE RETURNED A CONFIDENT ANSWER ABOUT ITS OWN REACH RATHER THAN ABOUT THE DATA, and every one was caught by asking what the instrument could not see rather than by re-reading its output.
NEITHER ITEM 7 NOR ITEM 11 IS CREDITED. Item 11 has two ids left and both need the client; item 7 needs fresh gameplay on both routes. Items 4d and 14 are credited and closed. ### LL-0108 - 2026-09-01 - 212 = Fervor, bound by SLOT ATTRIBUTION on a second Affix Details panel no session had opened - and a bare set difference would have published the wrong name

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1390 passed', exit 0, run as its own command immediately before commit
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0, run as its own command
- exactly TWO Affix Details window-open events exist in the whole corpus and BOTH have full-scene frame coverage: local 22:28:10 and 22:54:52
- f0119_22.28.15 read at native resolution: Fervid Lv.2 pants+boots, Fervor Lv.2 helm+chest, Seeker Lv.1 weapon, Wealth Lv.1 necklace
- f0124_22.54.52 read at native resolution, NEVER opened before: Fervid Lv.2 pants+boots, Ranged Lv.2 weapon+gloves, Fervor Lv.2 weapon+chest, Wealth Lv.1 necklace
- equipped set at local 22:53:30 is [1130308, 1230304, 1360303, 1430303, 1530303, 1630103, 1720201] in slot order; at 22:28:56 it is the same with 1330304 at gloves
- brace-balanced parse: 1230304 is slot 1 with item-borne affix 212 and NO gem; 1430303 is slot 3 and 1530303 slot 4, both affix 208; 1360303 is slot 2, affix 211; 1630103 is slot 5 with gem 224110 and no item-borne affix
- f0134_22.55.00 renders Fervor Lv.1 in the Deathclaw Hunter's Peridot gem row, and that item carries gem 223106 in the log
- f0073_22.26.34 renders Rover Hood, Rare Head, with Fervor Lv.1 in its gem row; that item is 1130308 at the helm slot, which carries gem 223106
- s01222_19.54.35 read: warehouse, a slot highlighted, NO tooltip - the recorded claim for the 101 event stands exactly as written

212 = Fervor, AND IT IS CONFIRMED TWICE ON TWO DIFFERENT LOADOUTS. The Affix Details screen prints a per-slot count per affix, and the second cfgId digit gives the slot, so a row attributes to a specific equipped item. On BOTH panels the CHEST slot contributes a Fervor, and on both the chest item is 1230304, which carries item-borne affix 212 and has no gem. Every other row on both panels is independently accounted for.
A BARE SET DIFFERENCE WOULD HAVE PUBLISHED THE WRONG NAME, AND MY FIRST PASS DID PICK IT. Counting affix INSTANCES rather than slots leaves {212, gem 223106, gem 224110} mapping onto {Fervor, Fervor, Wealth}, and TWO assignments satisfy the counts equally well. I had written 212 = Wealth and was reasoning toward publishing it. Only the per-slot counts break the tie, because they pin each contribution to a named slot and the log independently says which item sits in it. AN AGGREGATE THAT BALANCES IS NOT A BINDING - a conservation check confirms that nothing is missing, never which name goes where.
THE SECOND PANEL HAD NEVER BEEN OPENED BY ANY SESSION, and it was found by listing the window-open events rather than by looking where frames were expected. There are exactly two openings of that screen in the entire corpus and both are frame-covered - a two-of-two hit rate on a surface nobody had enumerated. THE CHEAPEST SWEEP IS OVER THE EVENT THAT OPENS A SCREEN, NOT OVER THE FRAMES THAT MIGHT SHOW IT.
A SEPARATE ID SPACE FELL OUT: GEMS. gem 223106 grants Fervor and gem 224110 grants Wealth. A gem contributes to the character exactly as an item-borne affix does, which is why one slot can carry two counts - the weapon column on the second panel shows both Ranged and Fervor, being its item-borne 211 plus its gem. The gem ids are NOT affix ids and must not be filed in the same table.
THE SECOND cfgId DIGIT IS THE EQUIPMENT SLOT: 11 helm, 12 chest, 13 gloves, 14 pants, 15 boots, 16 necklace, 17 ring, 30 weapon. POSITIVE-CONTROLLED rather than asserted: Fervid Lv.2 lands on the pants and boots columns, and the log independently puts affix 208 on 1430303 at slot 3 and 1530303 at slot 4. Two surfaces, the same two slots, neither fitted to the other.
209 = Seeker IS CONFIRMED A THIRD INDEPENDENT WAY. Yesterday's binding rested on elimination plus a durability corroboration. The first panel attributes Seeker Lv.1 to the weapon column while the log has 3030403, affix 209, in a weapon slot at that moment. A binding that survives three unrelated methods is as settled as this project's evidence gets.
101 AND 214 REMAIN, AND EACH IS NOW BLOCKED FOR A MEASURED REASON. 101 sits on 1430301 at the PANTS slot, which was held by 1430303 during both panel openings, so the panel route cannot see it. Its tooltip route fails more sharply than the recorded 'no tooltip was on screen': at its only frame-covered event the hover lasted 262 ms before the next item was hovered, against a capture running at about 1 frame per second. THE CAPTURE CADENCE, NOT THE RESOLUTION, IS WHAT LOSES IT - which is a different fix from the full-screen rule this project already adopted. 214 has no exEquip record, no durability record and appears on neither panel; it is known only from trade-filter requests and nothing on disk can reach it.
THE RECORDED CLAIM FOR THE 101 FRAME STANDS EXACTLY AS WRITTEN, and I checked rather than assumed. s01222_19.54.35 shows the warehouse with a slot highlighted and no tooltip, which is what docs/AFFIXES.md says. Having overturned a neighbouring generalisation yesterday, the temptation was to expect this one to fall too. It did not.
NOTHING WAS DONE TO THE GAME OR ITS DIRECTORY, the client was closed throughout, and no raw log excerpt is committed. The operator's persona appears in plain text beside the records used here and was kept out of every committed file, along with the 19-digit item instance ids.
ITEM 11 IS NOT CREDITED. Two affix ids remain unbound and both now have a measured blocker. It stays in flight.

### LL-0107 - 2026-09-01 - Item 14 CLOSED - its premise is refuted, the two bows are two TYPES sharing a name - and the refutation found a DURABILITY join that binds affix 209 = Seeker, an id three documents called unrecoverable from disk

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1390 passed', exit 0, run as its own command immediately before commit
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0, run as its own command
- brace-balanced parse of item records across 14 DISTINCT log contents: cfgId 3030403 slot 10, affix (209,1,true), durability 1166 and 1027; cfgId 3030404 slot 11, affix (211,1,true), durability 1137 and 1200; distinct 19-digit instance ids, held at once
- tier rule: max durability = 900 + (third cfgId digit) * 100, with every tier's largest observed value landing exactly on it - 1000, 1100, 1200, 1300, 1500 at tiers 1,2,3,4,6
- 1166/1200 = 97.17 percent renders as 97 percent; 1137/1200 = 94.75 percent renders as 94
- frame s01223_19.54.36 (25/scene, 1280x720) read at native resolution: Equipped Oil-soaked Wooden Bow, Rare Bow and Arrow, 23 Attack, +2.00 percent Physical Damage, Seeker Lv.1, durability 97 percent, Value 44
- frame f0134_22.55.00 read: Oil-soaked Wooden Bow under an Inventory header, Ranged Lv.1, 94 percent, Value 43, average transaction price 72
- frame f0130_22.28.38 read: the same bow name Equipped with Seeker Lv.1 at 63 percent
- among ALL durability records, exactly one WEAPON-range record renders as 97 percent and exactly one renders as 94 percent, so the join is unambiguous within that range
- tooltip-open to cfgId rate by world across all 14 logs: CampMap 1690 of 1690 = 100.0 percent, Whitewoods_Day 0 of 1246, Training 0 of 59
- AvgPrice cache is a cfgId to average-transaction-price table, 506 entries; 3030404 = 72, matching the 72 rendered in f0134_22.55.00

ITEM 14's PREMISE IS REFUTED AND THE ITEM CLOSES WITHOUT MEETING ITS ACCEPTANCE. It asked for two INSTANCES of one item type carrying different affixes. There were never two instances of one type: there are two different cfgIds, 3030403 and 3030404, with distinct instance ids in adjacent slots, both rendering as Oil-soaked Wooden Bow with identical base stats. LL-0096's own stated honest limit - that two cfgIds could share a name - is exactly what happened, and its refutation of the type-level model is therefore withdrawn. THE TYPE-TO-AFFIX MAPPING SURVIVES. What the episode does establish is narrower and still worth having: AN ITEM'S DISPLAY NAME DOES NOT IDENTIFY ITS TYPE, so any binding matched on a rendered name rather than a cfgId is unsafe.
A NEW JOIN KEY, AND IT IS THE REUSABLE PART. Item records carry a durability integer and the tooltip renders it as a percentage of a per-tier maximum, so a frame can be matched to a log record BY DURABILITY with no wall-clock coincidence at all. This project's whole id-binding method has been the wall-clock join; that join has a hard ceiling, because only 8 percent of tooltip cfgId events have any frame within two seconds. Durability has no such ceiling - the frame and the log line need not be contemporaneous.
209 = Seeker, AND THE ARGUMENT DOES NOT NEED THE DURABILITY. The frame shows a Rare Bow and Arrow carrying Seeker in the item-borne position. The log's affixed WEAPONS are 3030403 (209), 3030404 (211) and 3060404 (211). 211 = Ranged is confirmed three independent ways and the frame does not show Ranged, so it is neither of those; only 3030403 remains. Durability corroborates at 97 percent against 1166/1200. THE METHOD WAS POSITIVE-CONTROLLED BEFORE IT WAS TRUSTED: the same durability join on the 94 percent reading returns 3030404, whose affix 211 = Ranged is already known correct and is what that frame renders. A method that reproduces a known answer earned the right to produce a new one.
THE STATED LIMIT: the elimination assumes the bow on screen is one of the eight affixed cfgIds the log records, and a ninth affixed weapon never logged would break it. The durability record is also 77 minutes older than the frame, so it holds only if the bow was not used in between - which the 97 percent reading is itself evidence for. That is why the elimination is the primary argument and durability the corroboration, not the reverse.
A GENERALISATION IN docs/AFFIXES.md IS REFUTED, AND THE NARROW CLAIM UNDER IT SURVIVES. It read that 101 and 209 are lost because no usable tooltip was on screen AT ANY INSTANT a full-scene capture was running. At the four tooltip-EVENT timestamps that sweep checked, that is true. But a usable tooltip for 3030403 is on screen ONE SECOND after the 19:54:35 event it checked, and 33 seconds after the 19:54:03 event for the same item. WHY THE SWEEP COULD NOT HAVE FOUND IT BY LOOKING HARDER: it selected frames at the timestamps of log events, and a tooltip is rendered when the player HOVERS while the log line fires on its own schedule. Frame-selection by event time looks in the wrong second. The fix is not a wider window, it is a different key. That is LL-0100's rule turned on the sweep itself - the measurement was exact and answered a different question from the one that mattered.
I PUBLISHED A NUMERIC LAW ON TWO POINTS AND IT WAS WRONG. I claimed Value = base x durability with base 46 unique, from readings of 29 at 63 percent and 43 at 94 percent. A THIRD reading I had not found - 44 at 97 percent - kills it: 46 x 0.9717 = 44.70, which rounds to 45 where the screen says 44. Base 45 under ceiling fits all three, and each of the two item types yields 45 independently. THE DEEPER ERROR IS THAT n=2 WAS NEVER A TEST: two points exactly determine a line, so proportional and affine had zero degrees of freedom between them and my uniqueness claim was an artifact of having only two points. The law is now recorded as best-fitting at n=3, not as proven.
MY WORD ON THE TOOLTIP JOIN WAS RIGHT IN NUMBER AND WRONG IN DIAGNOSIS. I was going to publish that the tooltip-to-cfgId join is 'incomplete at about 70 percent'. Split by the world token in the open line it is not lossy at all: CampMap 1690 of 1690, Whitewoods_Day 0 of 1246, Training 0 of 59. AN AGGREGATE RATE HID AN OPERATIONAL RULE - capture item tooltips in CampMap and the join is total; capture them anywhere else and it is empty. That also explains cleanly why the window covering one of the two bow frames had five tooltip opens and no cfgId: all five were at world Training.
MY FRAME INDEX MISSED AN ENTIRE CAPTURE SET, which is how the third reading stayed hidden from me. I indexed filenames matching two conventions and there is a third: the 25/scene set uses an s-prefix with a jpg extension. THE SIXTH INSTRUMENT ERROR OF THIS SESSION, and every one of them was a pattern that returned a confident answer about its own reach rather than about the data. Enumerate conventions from the filesystem before trusting a filename filter.
NOTHING WAS DONE TO THE GAME OR ITS DIRECTORY and the client was closed throughout. No raw log excerpt is committed. The logs carry the operator's persona in plain text beside the records used here; it was read locally and kept out of every committed file, along with the 19-digit item instance ids, which are quoted nowhere.
ITEM 11 GAINS A BINDING AND A METHOD: three unbound affix ids remain, 101, 212 and 214. The same durability route can reach 101 on item 1430301 at 98 or 100 percent and 212 on 1230304 at 94, 98 or 100 percent, but PREFER A DISTINCTIVE PERCENTAGE - 97 picked out one weapon, while 100 is shared by many items and 98 is shared by those two. 214 has no durability record and is unreachable this way.

### LL-0106 - 2026-09-01 - Item 7 route 1 EXHAUSTED across the whole save corpus - and the refutation found a THIRD spelling of the damage field carrying a per-hit log surface nobody had enumerated, which the words 'nothing further can be extracted from disk' would have buried

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1390 passed', exit 0, run as its own command immediately before commit
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0, run as its own command
- 353 .sav files scanned across the capture tree and the live Saved tree, ZERO parse failures
- only StandaloneSlot carries DamageCollectonDataSet; the 7 other save types, 90 files, never carry it
- of 263 transient generations: 1 ABSENT, 10 present-and-EMPTY, 252 with records
- 424 child hit readings; nameId 0 on all 424 and Key empty on all 424, by TWO independent instruments - the repo reader, and a regex over the RAW JSON text that bypasses the hit dataclass entirely
- instrument proved non-vacuous: the same pass re-derived 262 carrying, 21 distinct hits, 1020.344 s, 9 instances, the same 8 monsterIds, total 1284.835785, range 9.745483 to 137.517426, and both the 278 top-level and 424 child counts
- new constants: sourceType 1 on all 278, bDeathCauser False on all 278, bChildDeathCauser False on all 424
- verbose log surface: 44 raw occurrences over 8 archived logs, deduplicating by their own log timestamp to 10 distinct events, 7 distinct damage values, 8 distinct insId values
- skillNameId re-derived across 25 logs -> still ZERO; but 139 save generations carry the token SkillNameId together with the int32 6130017, bytes 61895d00
- no damage value on the log side matches any value in the save corpus; the two event sets are 17 days apart, save run 2026-08-09 17:28-17:45 UTC against verbose events 2026-08-26

ROUTE 1 IS EXHAUSTED AND THE NEGATIVE HOLDS. No non-zero nameId exists anywhere in the damage data on this machine - not in 424 JSON readings, not in the 10 verbose events found later. Two instruments agreed and an independent refutation pass, using a third method that never grepped the property name at all, agreed again. Recorded so the search is never repeated.
THE INSTRUMENT WAS PROVED BEFORE THE NEGATIVE WAS BELIEVED, because a broken search and a true negative are indistinguishable. The scan re-derived eight figures this item already carried, including the 278 and 424 pair the item once confused for each other. A search that reproduces eight filed numbers is reading the data; one that reproduces none is reporting its own bug.
THE FRAMING AROUND THE NEGATIVE WAS FALSE AND WOULD HAVE BURIED A REAL SURFACE. The draft said 'nothing further can be extracted from disk' and 'closes a search nobody should repeat'. The refutation found a THIRD spelling of the field - nameid, all lowercase - in a non-JSON line neither of the two known spellings matches. 44 raw occurrences, 10 distinct events, 7 damage values, 8 insId values that distinguish individual monster instances. A TRUE NEGATIVE WRAPPED IN AN OVER-BROAD CONCLUSION IS MORE DANGEROUS THAN A FALSE POSITIVE, because it tells the next session not to look.
THE DIRECTION OF THE NEW SURFACE IS CONTESTED AND IS RECORDED AS UNSETTLED. The refuter read the 10 events as the operator's outgoing bow hits. The surrounding lines do not support that confidently: 2 of 7 in the largest log sit beside a hit-indicator bind and a bow ability End, and 2 of 7 sit beside a BeDamaged gameplay cue and an Adventurer hit line with a monster combo ability active. The verbose line carries NO sourceType, which is the field 7a identifies as the direction flag. BOTH READINGS ARE ADJACENCY ARGUMENTS. The right answer was not to flip one confident claim for another - it was to record the surface, record that its direction is undecided, and name what would settle it.
A DETERMINISM RESULT THAT DOES NOT DEPEND ON THE DIRECTION, and it is the most useful thing the new surface yields. The three-value sequence 16.99298095703125, 16.0489501953125, 75.52435302734375 occurs TWICE, against different monster instances, with gaps of 1.079 s then 0.278 s the first time and 1.083 s then 0.284 s the second - agreeing to about 5 ms, with the same structure of one instance followed by a second instance hit twice. That independently corroborates item 7's 'damage is DETERMINISTIC, not rolled' on a DIFFERENT emission surface and a DIFFERENT encounter. It is n=2 sequences and is not called a constant, the same hedge the item already applies to its own interval.
A LOG-SCOPED CLAIM WEARING CORPUS-SCOPED CLOTHES. Item 7 said 'no probe of the existing corpus can close' route 2. The corpus DOES contain a SkillNameId - 139 save generations carry 6130017 nested under LeaderRankScoreData, filed already in FINDINGS. What it does not contain is a SECOND DISTINCT value. Route 2 is still blocked and the reason is now stated correctly: a missing second value, not a missing field. My own draft made the mirror-image error, claiming 6130017 was no longer re-derivable because it is absent from the 25 LOGS - true of logs, false of disk.
THE 44-TO-10 COLLAPSE IS THE BYTE-PREFIX TRAP AGAIN, on a third quantity. Archived logs are prefixes of one another, so raw occurrence counts inflate: 44 raw lines are 10 events, and separately the log's 12 JSON attribution payloads are ONE payload seen 12 times. Counting either raw would have multiplied a single observation by four or twelve. Always deduplicate log evidence by something the game emitted, not by the file it was found in.
MY OWN INSTRUMENT PRODUCED A WRONG NUMBER AND IT WAS CAUGHT BEFORE PUBLICATION. A crude window scan for the first plausible int32 after the SkillNameId token reported 4539443 in 139 generations - it was reading type-name string bytes as an integer. Re-measured by matching the actual little-endian int32, the value is 6130017 in the same 139 generations. A HEURISTIC THAT ALWAYS RETURNS SOMETHING WILL RETURN SOMETHING WRONG RATHER THAN NOTHING.
NOTHING WAS DONE TO THE GAME OR ITS DIRECTORY, and no raw log excerpt is committed. The client was closed throughout. Player-instance ids, an actor name that may be a persona, and three GeoNames geolocation ids the refutation surfaced as operator PII were all kept out of every committed file and verified absent from the diff.
ITEM 7 IS NOT CREDITED. Both acceptance routes require fresh gameplay: route 1 is exhausted, route 2 needs a second distinct SkillNameId and the corpus holds exactly one. ITEM 11 IS ALSO STILL NOT CREDITED - it needs the client, which was closed all session.

### LL-0105 - 2026-09-01 - A process probe matched ITSELF and reported two watchers where one exists - the same self-match trap twice in one session, and the second time it nearly caused a wrong termination

**Evidence:**
- Get-CimInstance Win32_Process matched on CommandLine like '*lanternlight.armwatch*' -> 2 processes: pid 17568 and the probing pwsh itself
- the same query re-run with Name like 'python*' added -> 1 process, pid 17568
- that pid equals the one in ops/runtime/armwatch.json, so the record and reality agree
- the probing shell's own command line contains the search string, because the pattern is an argument to the command doing the searching
- post-push state re-verified: main e23f9fd matches origin/main, tree clean, loop state cycle 31 item 7 with 4d credited and 11 NOT credited

AN EMPTY GREP IS A CLAIM ABOUT YOUR PATTERN - AND SO IS A PROCESS PROBE. This repo already carried the rule for greps and for a NON-empty grep over log ids. This is the same defect against the PROCESS TABLE: any query that passes its pattern on a command line is visible to itself, so it can never report zero and will always over-count by one. It fired TWICE in this session - once on the opening 'is the client running' check, where it was caught immediately, and once at the wrap, where it looked like the second-watcher condition item 4d exists to prevent.
THE SECOND TIME IT NEARLY CAUSED A DESTRUCTIVE ACTION. The wrap read '2 armwatch processes' and the correct-looking response was to end the unrecorded one with taskkill. Investigating first showed the extra process was the probing shell. Nothing was terminated. THE RULE THAT SAVED IT is the same one that governs claims: measure what you think you are measuring BEFORE acting on it, and a probe that can see itself is not measuring the population you asked about.
THE FIX IS TO CONSTRAIN THE PROCESS NAME, not to refine the command-line pattern: match Name like 'python*' AND the command line, or exclude the current process id. A better pattern still matches the probe; a name filter cannot.
WHY THIS IS NOT A DEFECT IN 4d's REFUSAL: ensure_armed does not scan the process table at all. It reads the recorded pid and asks whether that pid is alive, which is immune to this entirely. The over-count existed only in the human-facing verification, which is exactly where a wrong reading turns into a wrong action.
NOTHING WAS KILLED AND NOTHING NEEDED TO BE. Exactly one watcher runs, pid 17568, archiving into C:/ll-captures/2026-09-01, and it is the pid the record names.

### LL-0104 - 2026-09-01 - Item 4d CLOSED - arming is wired into every session-entry document and pinned by a test, the dated destination rolls over without re-copying, and two refutation passes caught a RED suite and two wrong cost figures that this entry had already published

**Evidence:**
- python -m pytest -> '1390 passed in 25.79s', exit 0, its own command, run AFTER every correction below
- python -m ruff check . -> 'All checks passed!', exit 0, run as its own command
- collect summed per file -> 1390 across 29 files; baseline 1338 measured BEFORE dispatch
- merge gate over the 8 changed paths, baseline=1338 -> 'merge gate: OK (1390 tests collected)'
- MistfallHunter process count 0; live log unchanged at 235,864 bytes since 2026-08-30 21:11:46
- watcher armed LIVE as pid 17568, 'python -m lanternlight.armwatch --dest-base C:/ll-captures', created 2026-09-01 07:50:14 local
- its first pass archived 13 files / 13,019,536 bytes, NTFS CreationTime 07:50:14 on all 13
- a SECOND ensure_armed returned armed False naming pid 17568, spawned nothing, and left the record file mtime untouched; the machine still held exactly one armwatch process
- rollover driven with an INSTRUMENTED clock against real files: unchanged source produced no day-2 directory at all across 3 post-midnight passes; a changed source landed in 2026-09-02/
- capture tree 19,175 files / 9.884 GB, of which watcher snapshots - files named <stamp>_<size>_<name> - are 405 files / 128,619,411 bytes / 122.66 MB, 1.21 percent

THE REFUTATION FOUND THE SUITE RED WHILE THIS ENTRY ALREADY CLAIMED IT GREEN, and that is the most useful thing recorded here. The gates were run, then the documents were written, then the entry was appended citing the earlier run - and the doc prose itself broke the source-register test on 8 host-shaped tokens. Causation was proven rather than assumed: the committed versions of those documents contain none of the eight. A GREEN RESULT EXPIRES THE MOMENT YOU EDIT ANYTHING, INCLUDING PROSE. The repo's own directive listed that exact trap and it was still walked into, because the trap was filed under 'writing docs' while the gate was filed under 'finishing code'. It then went red a SECOND time, on a different token, after a further doc edit. RUN THE GATE IMMEDIATELY BEFORE COMMITTING, NOT BEFORE WRITING THE LEDGER.
THE CENTRAL CLAIM WAS OVERCLAIMED AND IS WITHDRAWN. An earlier draft said arming was 'bolted to the one step a cycle cannot skip'. It is not. The guard's released() context manager does not arm, so a cycle that takes the lock and nothing else still runs unwatched. What is true is weaker and is what the documents now say: arming is the documented default in every session-entry path, and test_every_session_entry_document_still_wires_the_arming pins all three documents so the wiring cannot vanish silently. The gain is SALIENCE plus a regression guard, not mechanism. Making the lock itself arm was considered and rejected - it would spawn a real watcher from inside every test that takes a lock.
A GAP IN THE WIRING, found by asking a refuter what a COLD session would be misled by rather than whether the code worked: the /continue command - which CLAUDE.md names as the cold-resume path - armed nothing at all, because only the loop path had been wired. It now arms. The pinning test was written first and watched to fail on exactly that one document while the other two passed, which is what makes it a discriminator rather than decoration.
THE COST FIGURE WAS WRONG TWICE, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, AND THE SECOND ERROR IS THE INSTRUCTIVE ONE. Published first as '9.84 MB/day, 3.51 GB/yr, 45x': the numerator counted only the 3 files in the live Logs directory while the denominator counted every surface, an asymmetry that UNDERSTATED the defect being argued against. Corrected to 12.42 MB/day and 4.43 GB/yr - one full startup pass per day - the denominator was then found too small as well, because it was built by filtering on DIRECTORY NAMES and silently missed 2026-08-25/saved-root, 7 files under an older hyphenated spelling of the same surface. The scope is now defined by the snapshot naming convention itself, which cannot drift with a directory name. AN EMPTY GREP IS A CLAIM ABOUT YOUR PATTERN - and so is a NON-empty one. Final: 12.42 MB/day, 4.43 GB/yr, roughly 37x the 122.66 MB the watchers have produced in total to date.
AN INTERNAL CONTRADICTION THE SAME TABLE CONTAINED: its per-day figures summed to more than the total printed two rows above them, which is exactly the arithmetic that exposes a scope error. They now sum to the total exactly, and that check is written into the table so the next author repeats it. A FILED COUNT IS A HYPOTHESIS - including one filed an hour ago by the session still writing the document.
MEASURING THE COST IS WHAT PRODUCED THE DESIGN, which is why the item asked for a rate rather than a proof. The first cut rebuilt each watcher at midnight, forgetting the (name, size, mtime_ns) identities it had captured and re-copying every unchanged file into every new dated directory. The arithmetic killed it before it shipped. A COST MEASURED ONLY AFTER SHIPPING WOULD HAVE BEEN A CAVEAT IN A DOCSTRING INSTEAD OF A DEFECT THAT NEVER LANDED. The caveat it does buy is stated rather than implied: a dated directory now holds what CHANGED that day, so reconstructing a surface on a quiet day means reading back to the last directory that captured it.
TWO VACUITY GAPS ARE DISCLOSED RATHER THAN CLAIMED AWAY, both found by mutation in a mirror checkout. Gutting the real detached-spawn helper so it returns a pid without starting anything leaves the whole suite GREEN - that production path has no test, by deliberate choice, because a test that exercised it would leave a real poller running against the operator's save directory after the suite exits. And the no-termination guard walks the AST for call NAMES, so a kill smuggled through a subprocess argument list would pass it. Neither is fixed here; both are now written down, because the surrounding prose reads stronger than the guards actually are.
FIVE DOCUMENTS WERE LEFT DESCRIBING THE OLD BEHAVIOUR AND ARE NOW CORRECTED. Item 4c still said NO WATCHER IS RUNNING and that arming was owed with TODAY's date - the literal dated path this very item exists to remove - two hundred lines above the new text in the same file. ARCHITECTURE and README still said arming was manual with no date logic. Both hand-off documents still ended at 2026-08-31. That is LL-0103's finding repeating one session later: FIXING THE RECORDS YOU HAPPENED TO OPEN IS NOT FIXING THE RECORDS A COLD SESSION READS.
LL-0100's RULE EARNED ITS KEEP FIVE TIMES, and twice against this session's own verification. Recorded claims nearly corrected falsely: (1) the archive holds 19 game logs where LL-0101 recorded 22 - scope, not shrinkage, being 19 archived plus 3 live, with the distinct-hash count matching at 14 exactly; (2) a grep reported every hand-off document as CRLF, contradicting LL-0103, but a binary read confirmed LL-0103 precisely, so the grep was a claim about the tool; (3) LL-0101 recorded a backup 'created at 21:11:19' while the only candidate file is named and mtime-stamped 06:24:23 UTC - the NTFS CreationTime is 21:11:19 exactly, so the filename carries the SOURCE log's time, not the backup moment. All three stand as written. Then twice against my own harness while checking the no-duplicate-on-rollover claim: the first called the entry point TWICE, which is two separate armings each starting with an empty seen-set, and 'measured' a duplicate the design does not produce; the second clamped a two-tick fake clock so pass one already believed it was day two. Only an instrumented clock that logged every call showed the real shape - one call at arming plus one per pass. AN EXACT MEASUREMENT OF THE WRONG SETUP LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A REFUTATION.
ONE PART OF THIS ITEM RESTS ON A HARNESS, NOT AN OBSERVATION, and the documents now say so: no watcher has yet been seen crossing a REAL midnight. The rollover is driven with an injected clock against real files on disk, which is strong evidence and is not the same thing. The live observations are the derived destination, the archiving, and the refusal.
A TRAP EARNED, and it executed a stray script. In this Git Bash TMPDIR is UNSET, so a path written as "$TMPDIR/<name>" expands to /<name>, which MSYS maps to C:/Program Files/Git/<name>. A real scratch script another session had left there the same way, dated 2026-08-29, was executed as a result. It crashed on its own backup step before mutating anything and the repo was verified untouched, with only the intended paths differing. Quote the variable or check it is set: an unset prefix does not fail, it silently relocates the path to the Git install root, and that directory is collecting other sessions' debris that is not ours to delete.
FOUR TOKENS WERE ADDED TO THE SOURCE REGISTER'S NON-HOST LIST and every one is a real file or module in this repo, looked at before adding, exactly as that list's own contract requires. The other six red tokens were REWRITTEN OUT OF THE PROSE instead - citing code as a path in backticks - because that denylist is how a real source once hid and it should grow only for things demonstrably not sources.
NOTHING WAS DONE TO THE GAME OR ITS DIRECTORY. The client was closed all session and was not started, the watcher copies out and never writes to the source, and no game process was touched. Nothing was killed: the supervisor has no terminate path, and liveness goes through the guard module's pid_is_alive helper because the conventional POSIX signal-0 existence probe on Windows maps onto TerminateProcess.
ITEM 11 IS NOT CREDITED. It needs the client, which was closed all session, and nothing was measured for it. The four affix ids 101, 209, 212 and 214 remain unbound. The Splatter Arrow tooltip and one of Elusive, Smiting or Curse are both still owed and both need a hover.
